import torch
import numpy as np

class SATWhirligigEngine(torch.nn.Module):
    """
    SAT WHIRLIGIG TENSOR ENGINE (June 2026 Build)
    Implements 4D Superhelical Particle Path Lagrangian (4DSHPPL) mechanics.
    Computes 4th-order worldline bending energy, dynamic hypersphere constraints (UI),
    and inter-curve coupling using 4D autodiff.
    """
    def __init__(self, kappa=1.0, lambda_s=100.0, k_coupling=10.0):
        super(SATWhirligigEngine, self).__init__()
        self.kappa = torch.tensor(kappa, dtype=torch.float32)         # Filament stiffness / resistance to kinking
        self.lambda_s = torch.tensor(lambda_s, dtype=torch.float32)   # Lagrange multiplier for UI S^3 constraint
        self.k = torch.tensor(k_coupling, dtype=torch.float32)       # Inter-filament coupling stiffness

    def compute_explicit_superhelix_path(self, lambda_param, R_lambda, r_h, omega_s, omega_h, phase_offsets):
        """
        Explicitly constructs H^μ(λ) = (x, y, z, t) for a 4D superhelix winding along an expanding hypersphere.
        """
        phi_0, psi_0 = phase_offsets
        
        # xy-plane: Large-scale spatial winding (Symmetry Axis)
        x = R_lambda * torch.cos(omega_s * lambda_param + phi_0)
        y = R_lambda * torch.sin(omega_s * lambda_param + phi_0)
        
        # zw-plane: High-frequency local coiling (Torsion Anchor)
        z = r_h * torch.cos(omega_h * lambda_param + psi_0)
        t = r_h * torch.sin(omega_h * lambda_param + psi_0)
        
        return torch.stack([x, y, z, t], dim=-1)

    def evaluate_whirligig_lagrangian(self, H, H_prime, H_prime_prime, R_lambda, G_path):
        """
        Computes L_total = L_filament + L_UI + L_proj
        """
        # 1. Filament Bending Energy: Squared magnitude of the second derivative
        L_filament = 0.5 * self.kappa * torch.sum(H_prime_prime ** 2, dim=-1)
        
        # 2. Universal Indicatrix Hypersphere Embedding Constraint
        h_norm_sq = torch.sum(H ** 2, dim=-1)
        L_UI = 0.5 * self.lambda_s * ((h_norm_sq - R_lambda**2) ** 2)
        
        # 3. Inter-Curve Coupling (Projection / Precession Vector field)
        L_proj = 0.5 * self.k * torch.sum((H - G_path) ** 2, dim=-1)
        
        L_total = L_filament + L_UI + L_proj
        return L_total, L_filament, L_UI, L_proj

    def verify_fourth_order_euler_lagrange(self, lambda_val, path_fn, R_fn, G_fn):
        """
        Autodiff verification of the fourth-order mechanical response constraint:
        κ H_i^{(4)} + 2λ_s(r^2 - R^2)H_i + k(H_i - G_i) = 0
        """
        lam = lambda_val.clone().detach().requires_grad_(True)
        
        # Reconstruct path and dependencies as a function of the parameter lambda
        H = path_fn(lam)
        R = R_fn(lam)
        G = G_fn(lam)
        
        # First Derivatives (Velocity Trace)
        H_prime = torch.stack([torch.autograd.grad(H[i], lam, create_graph=True)[0] for i in range(4)])
        
        # Second Derivatives (Curvature / Bending Acceleration)
        H_prime_prime = torch.stack([torch.autograd.grad(H_prime[i], lam, create_graph=True)[0] for i in range(4)])
        
        # Third Derivatives (Torsional Jerk)
        H_3 = torch.stack([torch.autograd.grad(H_prime_prime[i], lam, create_graph=True)[0] for i in range(4)])
        
        # Fourth Derivatives (Structural Snap Vector)
        H_4 = torch.stack([torch.autograd.grad(H_3[i], lam, create_graph=True)[0] for i in range(4)])
        
        # Evaluate left-hand equation residuals per coordinate axis
        r_sq = torch.sum(H ** 2)
        ui_restoration = 2.0 * self.lambda_s * (r_sq - R**2) * H
        coupling_restoration = self.k * (H - G)
        
        eq_of_motion_residue = (self.kappa * H_4) + ui_restoration + coupling_restoration
        return eq_of_motion_residue, H_prime_prime

## Frontier 1: The Pauli Horizon Idea (Micro-Scale Relativistic Freeze)
To audit the conjecture that the Pauli Exclusion Principle is an asymptotic coordinate freeze caused by localized gravitational time dilation, we must look directly at the metric-generating mechanism of the Universal Indicatrix.
## The Geometric Generation
In SAT, the emergent spacetime metric $g_{\mu\nu}$ is not an independent background grid; it is constructed from the averaged tangents of the 4D worldtube filaments ($u_\mu$) scaled by the structural snap constant ($B$):
$$g_{\mu\nu} = \eta_{\mu\nu} + B \cdot u_\mu u_\nu$$ 
Where $\eta_{\mu\nu} = \text{diag}(-1, 1, 1, 1)$ is the flat Minkowski baseline.
For a single rest-state particle traveling perfectly parallel to the time normal, the preferred velocity vector is $u_\mu = (1, 0, 0, 0)$. Plugging this in yields the temporal metric component:
$$g_{00} = -1 + B(1)(1) = -1 + B$$ 
Since $B \approx 0.2387$, $g_{00} \approx -0.7613$. This negative value guarantees a stable, forward-propagating temporal flow.
## The Boundary Event Horizon Condition
Now, bring a second fermion filament close to the first on the active 3D timesheet surface. As their microscopic event horizon worldtubes begin to overlap, their localized 4D velocity vectors superimpose.
The Pauli Exclusion limit is achieved when the physical spacing approaches the transverse filament radius ($\Delta x \to \ell_f$). At this boundary, the localized additive coiling tension forces the temporal component $g_{00}$ to vanish completely:
$$\lim_{\Delta x \to \ell_f} g_{00} = -1 + B \cdot \left( u_0^{(1)} + u_0^{(2)} \right)^2 \to 0$$ 
## The Architectural Audit

* Why it Works: In General Relativity, as $g_{00} \to 0$, the coordinate time interval $dt/d\lambda$ blows up to infinity. Time stops. From the perspective of the advancing 3D timesheet, the two particles can never cross into the exact same spatial coordinates because they are asymptotically frozen in each other's local gravity wells.
* Why True Bosons Escape: True Bosons in SAT are transient timesheet excitations (waves or ripples) rather than persistent 4D filament worldtubes. Because they lack a persistent $u_\mu$ vector, they do not modify the local metric tensor via this outer product mechanism. They generate no localized $g_{00}$ freeze, which is why they do not obey the Pauli Exclusion Principle and can occupy the same space freely.

## Workbench 1: The Pauli Horizon Spacetime Freeze ($g_{00} \to 0$)
To derive the Pauli Exclusion Principle as a relativistic coordinate freeze rather than an abstract quantum mechanical rule, we must model what happens when two persistent fermion worldtubes approach a shared 3D spatial position on the advancing timesheet wavefront.
## 1. Defining the Overlapping Velocity Fields
In the SAT framework, the emergent spacetime metric tensor $g_{\mu\nu}$ is generated by the outer product of the localized filament tangent vectors ($u_\mu$), which track the preferred direction of cosmic time-flow:
$\begin{equation} g_{\mu\nu}(x) = \eta_{\mu\nu} + B \cdot u_\mu(x) u_\nu(x) \end{equation}$
Where $\eta_{\mu\nu} = \text{diag}(-1, 1, 1, 1)$ is the flat Minkowski baseline, and $B \approx 0.23873241$ is the foundational projection constant.
When a single, isolated fermion filament is at rest relative to the universal radial expansion ($c$), its velocity vector aligns perfectly with the time normal:
